## Motion Authoring, Editing & Retargeting

- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.

## Character Motion Synthesis & Animation

- 2026-11-16, **DuoFlow: Towards Synchronized Speech and Motion Generation with Fine-Grained Control**, Jun Dong et.al., Paper: [@2611.21530](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21530)
> **Abstract:** An embodied speaker's expressiveness depends not only on what is said, but also on how the voice sounds and how the body moves. However, existing joint speech-motion generation models are largely driven implicitly by transcript semantics or intermediate speech representations, offering little explicit control over motion hierarchy, body parts, or vocal style. To bridge this gap, we construct MotionSpeech, a structured benchmark for controllable joint speech-motion generation that includes transcripts, voice-style descriptions, and motion conditions at two hierarchical levels. Building on this benchmark, we propose DuoFlow, a dual-stream latent flow transformer that jointly denoises speech and motion under a shared noise level and a unified second-based temporal coordinate. Modality-specific residual connectors inject structured conditions into the corresponding streams, layer-wise bidirectional cross-modal attention coordinates their generation, and a monotonic phoneme pathway combines normalized progress anchors with CTC supervision to strengthen speech temporal modeling. DuoFlow provides a unified interface for fine-grained motion and vocal-style control and demonstrates the ability to synthesize high-quality, synchronized, and instruction-consistent speech-motion sequences.
- 2026-11-15, **SGIP: Learning Scene-Grounded Interaction Priors for Scene-Aware Human Motion Prediction**, Donghyeon Soon et.al., Paper: [@2611.20076](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20076)
> **Abstract:** Human motion prediction (HMP) aims to forecast future movement from an observed motion sequence, and recent methods have incorporated 3D scene geometry and object semantics to improve long-term plausibility. However, the destination of future motion remains ambiguous in cluttered scenes: the same observed motion can lead to different interaction targets or navigation modes. Existing methods either handle this ambiguity implicitly or rely on auxiliary signals like gaze that are hard to obtain in practice. To address this, we propose SGIP, which explicitly predicts where in the scene future motion will be grounded across both objects and open navigation regions, and uses it to condition future trajectory and full-body pose prediction. This scene-grounded interaction prior is learned via intent distillation: at training time only, a teacher model scores candidate scene regions by combining an LLM prompted with a textual description of the person's intent with geometric motion-scene features. A student model learns to reproduce these scores from only the observed motion and 3D scene, making intent and LLM unnecessary at test time. Across multiple benchmark datasets, SGIP outperforms prior scene-aware baselines on both trajectory and pose accuracy in a realistic deployment setting where neither gaze nor intent is available at inference, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed framework. The project page is available at https://sgip2026.github.io.
- 2026-11-14, **Spectral Steering: Local Inverse Geometry for Frozen Diffusion Generators**, Angelo Moroncelli et.al., Paper: [@2611.19572](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19572)
> **Abstract:** A frozen diffusion generator may need to change several output properties while preserving others. We study when guidance faithfully realizes such a structured request and whether local inverse correction improves its direction. Our account links this benefit to the interaction between the request and the objective-specific remaining-sampler Jacobian: conditioning alone is insufficient. Spectral steering combines a request-specific cosine-gain predictor with damped inverse correction and fixed-time relinearization for finite edits. Scalar nulls and matched-movement controls test the predicted mechanism. Across 8,923 instances in a retrospectively identified local regime, predicted and realized gains correlate at Spearman 0.79, versus -0.11 for condition number. Experiments span images, audio, robotics, and control and planning, including complex character motion generation. On 50 paired inpainting cases, four corrections improve unobserved-pixel PSNR over Adam by 3.25 dB at equal total derivative traversals. Together, the analysis and controls connect request-dependent geometry to directional gains and establish where these gains translate into downstream performance.
- 2026-11-14, **ImpedanceMimic: Learned Impedance as Action Space for Force-Accurate Motion Imitation**, Matthias Jammot et.al., Paper: [@2611.19153](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19153)
> **Abstract:** Recovering the forces behind a motion is ill-posed: different controllers can reproduce the same movement while generating different contact forces. Physics-based motion imitation resolves this ambiguity through simulation, but relies on fixed-gain proportional-derivative (PD) control. We show that fixed gains impose a trade-off between motion and force tracking, as contact and swing place conflicting demands on joint stiffness and damping. We introduce ImpedanceMimic, a reinforcement-learning policy that modulates per-joint stiffness, damping, and position setpoints from motion and force measurements, but requires only motion at inference. Across 129 participants and six activities, ImpedanceMimic outperforms matched fixed-gain PD under every integration scheme, direct-torque, muscle-actuated and existing imitation controllers in kinematic and force fidelity, approaching a dedicated motion-to-force regressor. By expressing gains as multiples of inertia-scaled priors, a single policy controls subject-specific biomechanical models without explicit anthropometric input and transfers zero-shot to unseen humans and a humanoid robot. The learned impedance forms a low-dimensional representation of motor state, shared across bodies, that generalizes to withheld activities: stiffness scales with load, and damping anticipates unloading.
- 2026-11-12, **Ropedia Xperience 2: A Gold Standard for Scaling Physical AI Environments and Behaviors**, Hao Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.17060](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17060)
> **Abstract:** Large foundation models such as GPT-6 Astra and Claude Fable 5.1 have recently been shown to control robots directly from visual observations. They nevertheless remain brittle on long-horizon, contact-rich tasks in real-world environments. Further progress depends on two complementary resources: demonstrations of human behavior that supervise how tasks are performed, and interactive environments in which policies can be rolled out, evaluated, and improved in closed loop. Language models have advanced along well-characterized scaling laws; the corresponding question for physical AI is how capability scales with environments and behaviors, which first requires identifying which data to scale and how to produce and reuse such data continuously. To address this question, we introduce Ropedia Xperience 2, comprising 1,589 released metric-scale 3D scans and a collection of 4,230 egocentric recordings of 74 tasks (131 hours). We further show that each capture can be repurposed for robot learning: scenes are converted into physically interactive simulation environments, human motion is retargeted to whole-body robot trajectories, and additional robot behaviors are obtained through virtual teleoperation and automated long-horizon trajectory generation. We propose this joint capture of environments and behaviors as a gold standard for physical AI data collection, laying a data foundation for establishing scaling laws over environments and behaviors.
- 2026-11-10, **EgoHMP: Achieving Precise Human Motion Prediction in 3D Scenes via Egocentric Cues**, Xin Zhao et.al., Paper: [@2611.13654](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13654)
> **Abstract:** The complex interactive dependencies between human behavior and 3D scenes make accurately predicting human intent a major challenge for human motion prediction (HMP). While recent studies use human gaze to infer intent, gaze signals suffer from ambiguity, drift, and a reliance on eye-tracking hardware. Research in cognitive science indicates that egocentric views inherently encode potential interactions, while head motion serves as a crucial precursor in motion planning. Motivated by this, we propose EgoHMP, a novel framework that uses egocentric views and head motions as robust carriers of interaction intent. By translating this intent into an envisioning of future interactions, EgoHMP achieves precise HMP in 3D scenes. EgoHMP first employs a vision-motion alignment encoder to align the visual and motion feature spaces. Based on these aligned features, an egocentric intent predictor with a modality-aware modulation mechanism adaptively suppresses invalid interaction cues to predict an accurate interaction probability map. Furthermore, to enhance the stability and realism of the generated motions, we propose a contact-aware motion decoder. Specifically, inspired by the human cognitive process of planning trajectories prior to action execution, we first predict a global trajectory to serve as a self-prompt, effectively mitigating the optimization conflict in diffusion models. Subsequently, we introduce a multi-stage denoiser, which relieves motion artifacts by incorporating human contact modeling through a multi-stage optimization strategy. Finally, a GCN-based motion decoder is employed to synthesize physically plausible and semantically consistent motions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that EgoHMP achieves state-of-the-art performance.
- 2026-11-10, **FlashMotion: Ultra-Fast Long-Term 3D Motion Generation**, Kaixing Yang et.al., Paper: [@2611.13626](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13626)
> **Abstract:** 3D human motion generation plays a crucial role in game development, extended reality (XR), digital humans, and embodied simulation. Beyond motion generation, practical pipelines include rigging, retargeting, and rendering, making ultra-fast long-term 3D motion generation without compromising quality valuable for real-time interactive applications. Despite promising progress, high-quality long-horizon motion generation under tight inference budgets remains challenging, owing to 2 coupled bottlenecks: (1) generative strategies that require many sampling steps for high-quality 3D motion generation and (2) model architectures with substantial per-step computational and memory costs for long sequences. We present FlashMotion, a co-designed framework for high-quality long-horizon 3D motion generation under a strict inference budget, covering text-to-motion, music-to-dance, and speech-to-gesture tasks. To enhance trajectory-level efficiency, we introduce a 3D motion-space MeanFlow formulation with kinematic and dynamic consistency constraints for few-step generation, together with an Adaptive Soft masking mechanism for temporally coherent motion editing. To improve per-step efficiency, we design a lightweight non-autoregressive BiMamba architecture with condition-guided state selection and AdaIN-based condition fusion mechanism, enhancing cross-modal alignment and generalization to longer motion sequences. Extensive experiments demonstrate that FlashMotion achieves overall state-of-the-art performance in both quality and efficiency across text-to-motion, speech-to-gesture, and music-to-dance generation. The project page is available at https://sun-happy-ykx.github.io/FlashMotion-Project/
- 2026-11-09, **LHNav: Long-Horizon Humanoid Navigation via Multirate Coordination and Physics-Grounded Action Data**, Xueyang Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.12136](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12136)
> **Abstract:** We study humanoid navigation across multiple destinations under long-horizon language instructions. This task is challenging because robots must maintain progress across successive goals while continuously producing natural, stable whole-body motion through confined spaces. However, task decomposition in existing methods does not by itself ensure progress throughout execution, while waypoint-based navigation does not guarantee natural, route-faithful whole-body motion. Our key idea is to organize navigation into three complementary roles operating at different time scales: a Brain for task reasoning, a Cerebellum for local motion generation, and a Brainstem for whole-body execution. We realize this idea in LHNav, an asynchronous system that uses progress and arrival feedback to coordinate navigation goals, VLA motion-token prediction, and whole-body control. To train the Cerebellum for natural, route-faithful motion, we develop a physics-grounded data engine that improves physical tracking of motion references along collision-aware routes and pairs the resulting demonstrations with rendered visual observations. Together, these designs allow task planning to proceed alongside continuous movement and extend single-goal navigation to successive destinations with natural, route-faithful whole-body motion. In simulation, our navigation VLA outperforms OmniNav and DualVLN in single-goal success and motion smoothness, with fewer collisions and falls. Using a VLA trained only on single-goal demonstrations, LHNav achieves a 75.56% success rate on long-horizon navigation tasks. Deployment on the Unitree G1 further demonstrates single-goal navigation and zero-shot long-horizon task execution in real-world environments, including layouts with repositioned objects. Project website: https://robbyant-research.github.io/LHNav/.
- 2026-11-08, **Kimodo-Policy: Learning Kinematic Action Models for Humanoid Loco-Manipulation**, Yunheng Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.11554](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11554)
> **Abstract:** Humanoid loco-manipulation requires continuous coordination of high-dimensional whole-body motion to realize task objectives. While vision-language-action models and world action models transfer semantic and visual-dynamics priors to robot control, these priors do not necessarily capture the structure of robot motion. We introduce Kinematic Action Models (KAMs), a paradigm that treats whole-body motion as a direct pretraining target and uses a pretrained motion generator as the primary action-generation backbone. KAMs ground learned temporal and cross-body coordination priors through task conditioning, visual observations, and execution feedback. We present Kimodo-Policy as an initial instantiation, combining a frozen Kimodo text-to-motion backbone with lightweight ControlNet-based visual conditioning. A root-body-hand hierarchy coordinates locomotion and manipulation, while motion-history conditioning and repeated action-chunk prediction bridge motion synthesis and closed-loop control. Following large-scale loco-manipulation pretraining, the policy achieves the highest overall success among compared policies on multi-task HumanoidArena (72.14%), competitive performance on SIMPLE, and 60% mean success across eight real-world tasks, with 678M parameters (92M trainable). Ablations support the contribution of the pretrained motion backbone. Together, these results provide initial evidence for motion generation as a complementary foundation for humanoid action learning.
- 2026-11-08, **InfCoiL: Coordinated Planner-Controller Learning for Closed-Loop Physics-Based Human-Object Interaction**, Yude Zou et.al., Paper: [@2611.11517](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11517)
> **Abstract:** Text- and goal-conditioned human-object interaction (HOI) provides an intuitive interface for specifying high-level intents in physics-based character animation. Mapping such intents to physically plausible, contact-rich behaviors naturally calls for a generative planner and a physics-based controller, yet existing planner-controller systems either execute plans open-loop or close the loop while keeping the planner fixed, so execution feedback never reshapes what is planned. We present InfCoiL, a closed-loop framework for physics-based HOI from coordinated planner-controller learning. InfCoiL couples a morphology-aware flow-matching planner with a unified multi-morphology controller through a shared morphology interface in an autoregressive plan-and-execute loop, and alternately fine-tunes both components with reinforcement learning from the same physical rollouts. To optimize the flow-matching planner online, we introduce Semantic Flow Policy Optimization (SFPO), which stochasticizes selected denoising transitions with channel-wise semantic covariance over heterogeneous HOI features and assigns group-relative, sequence-level rewards from complete closed-loop executions. On held-out OMOMO sequences, InfCoiL outperforms open-loop hierarchical baselines in goal-reaching task success and physical tracking accuracy. Project page: https://infcoil.github.io/
- 2026-11-08, **Does Pose Encoding Matter? Analyzing Geometric Representations for Discrete Motion Vocabulary Learning**, Amir Kimiyaie et.al., Paper: [@2611.11093](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11093)
> **Abstract:** Compressing continuous human motion into a finite vocabulary of discrete tokens is now the first stage of many motion understanding and generation pipelines. Vector quantization partitions a latent space into Voronoi cells around a finite codebook, but the geometry of that latent space is inherited from the input pose encoding, and it is unclear which encoding admits an efficient discretization. We study four encodings that place skeletal motion in different geometric spaces: Gaussian joint heatmaps on the pixel lattice, which we show to be a morphological dilation of the joint point set by a quadratic structuring function; root-relative joint positions in Euclidean space; local joint rotations on $\SO$ through a continuous 6D embedding; and a redundant full kinematic encoding. For each, we train VQ-VAE tokenizers whose codebooks lie on the unit hypersphere, varying codebook size, temporal stride, and code dimension. Beyond reconstruction, we measure codebook utilization and the effective vocabulary size, i.e., the number of equiprobable spherical Voronoi cells with the same entropy as the observed token distribution. Rotation and position encodings keep converting additional codewords into effective vocabulary, whereas the full kinematic encoding saturates at a few hundred effective cells and heatmaps at fewer than sixty. The smallest code dimension gives the highest utilization for three of the four encodings, and the effect of temporal stride depends on the encoding. Encodings of the same pose family can thus yield effective vocabularies differing by an order of magnitude, a difference that reconstruction loss alone does not reveal, which points to input geometry as a first-order design choice in motion tokenization.
- 2026-11-07, **MotionGraphicsBench: Evaluating Motion Graphics Generation with Programmatic Verification**, Jiaju Ma et.al., Paper: [@2611.10592](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10592)
> **Abstract:** Large language models can generate motion graphics from static images and natural language requests, but it remains difficult to evaluate how well models can follow requests that combine spatial, appearance, and temporal requirements. We introduce MotionGraphicsBench, an expert-curated benchmark of 100 instances for animating an input static SVG with a motion edit prompt. By supplying all required visual elements, our editing task reflects a designer-like workflow and isolates motion generation from graphic generation. Derived from real-world Lottie motion designs, our benchmark contains diverse combinations of spatio-temporal animation behaviors. Each benchmark instance pairs its model input with a deterministic, human-readable verification program that accepts different valid implementations and reports which prompt requirements pass or fail, rather than comparing against a reference animation or using a VLM judge. We construct these programs with a multi-agent pipeline using human-labeled animations and synthesized test cases. On a balanced test set of 180 animations, our programs achieve 93.3% raw agreement with human judgments, compared with 83.3% for a GPT-6 Astra VLM baseline, and outperform the baseline in both approval and rejection recall. Across 47 configurations from 32 LLMs and 12 providers, pass@1 ranges from 6.0% to 67.7%. Across 15 models evaluated at their highest available reasoning effort with 32 attempts per instance, additional attempts improve every model, but pass@32 still ranges from 38.0% to 95.0%. Our analysis of verification programs reveals that generated animations often fail to match reference poses, follow requested motion trajectories, or coordinate related events. We release our benchmark at https://huggingface.co/datasets/figma-ai/motion-graphics-bench.
- 2026-11-06, **Improving the Diffusability of Motion Tokenizer**, Guanhe Huang et.al., Paper: [@2611.10160](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10160)
> **Abstract:** Latent diffusion models paired with a variational auto-encoder (VAE) tokenizer have shown promising performance for efficient text-driven human motion generation. However, motion latent diffusion suffers from a generation-reconstruction trade-off: simply increasing tokenizer capacity enhances reconstruction but fails to proportionally improve text-conditioned generation quality. We assume this trade-off stems from a spectral mismatch inside the VAE tokenizer: the encoder yields a latent space biased toward high frequencies, and the decoder introduces temporal artifacts. In this paper, we propose the Diffusable Motion Tokenizer (DiMoT) to improve the diffusability of the tokenizer, rather than scaling up the diffusion backbone. To improve text-conditioned optimization, Critic Spectral Adaptation (CSA) leverages a pretrained diffusion model as the critic to suppress high-frequency latent energy. To preserve motion dynamics, the Alpha-Flow Motion Decoder (AMD) performs generative decoding via average-velocity prediction, enabling efficient one-step decoding. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DiMoT achieves state-of-the-art performance across various challenging text-to-motion benchmarks, yielding superior motion fidelity and text-motion alignment. Project page: https://githubhgh.github.io/dimot/.
- 2026-11-05, **EgoGeneralist: Unifying Egocentric Action-Video Tasks through Video-Native Modeling**, Yanlin Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.08477](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08477)
> **Abstract:** Modern video models possess strong visual priors, yet do not natively represent human action in metric 3D space. We present EgoGeneralist, which adapts pretrained video generators to unify action-conditioned video generation, pose estimation, and joint action-video generation for egocentric manipulation. Our role-adaptive action renderer provides video-native representations for multidirectional action-video learning, making metric motion usable as both a spatial control signal and a recoverable generation target. Cross-stream action-video coupling establishes close correspondence between metric motion and visual dynamics through lightweight adaptation while preserving the pretrained visual prior. Together, these designs establish a generalist framework spanning six action-video tasks. With only narrow-domain data, EgoGeneralist generalizes to diverse unseen scenes and interactions with effective action control and visual quality, bridging pretrained visual knowledge and metric 3D human motion.
- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.
- 2026-11-05, **IMUDINO: Self-distilled Foundation Model for Inertial Sensing**, Zhoutong Ye et.al., Paper: [@2611.07437](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07437)
> **Abstract:** We present IMUDINO, the first foundation model (FM) for inertial sensing to achieve competitive performance across diverse sensing tasks including human activity recognition (HAR), health, motion capture, and odometry. Prior inertial FMs often focus on one subfield of inertial sensing and sometimes underperform state-of-the-art supervised methods (e.g., in HAR), making their scope considerably narrower than FMs for vision and natural language. Moreover, they often require fixed input length and sampling rate, and generalize poorly to unseen sensor locations. To build a general-purpose foundation model for inertial sensing, we adapt the DINO self-supervised learning (SSL) paradigm to inertial data through a set of semantics-preserving motion transformations that simulate variations in motion pattern and sensor setup. For model architecture, we use a plain transformer encoder with frequency-aware positional encoding that natively supports varying input length and sampling rate. This simple setup proved remarkably effective for learning global and dense features generalizable across users, sensor setups, tasks, and, in the case of robot odometry, embodiments. We hope IMUDINO can serve as a backbone for inertial sensing and kickstart the development of general-purpose inertial FMs.
- 2026-11-04, **Semantic Residual Vector Quantization for Sign Language Generation**, Chang Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.06781](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06781)
> **Abstract:** Sign language generation (SLG) maps written sentences into signing motion while preserving both lexical content and fine-grained articulation. This requires a model to decide what signs should be expressed and to realize them through precise handshape, trajectory, facial expression and body coordination. Existing pose-based systems and reconstruction-only motion tokenizers often entangle these two decisions, leaving the generator without an explicit text-aligned intermediate target for what to sign before refining how to sign it. To address this issue, we introduce Semantic Residual Vector Quantization (SRVQ), a framework for SLG that frames generation as a two-level process moving from semantics to kinematics. The newly introduced SRV Quantizer learns a hierarchy in which the first residual level encodes sign content and subsequent levels refine pose, handshape, trajectory, and body coordination, all within a single quantizer and without separate semantic representations or external planning stages. Through word-aware conditioning for hierarchical code generation, a masked semantic generator predicts semantic codes and a kinematic generator completes the residual codes for full SMPL-X motion synthesis. We evaluate using retrieval and FID in a shared text-motion embedding space, alongside standard geometric metrics. On CSL-Daily and Phoenix-2014T, SRVQ achieves R@1 of 82.3 and 70.3, outperforming the previous SOTA by 30 and 46 points, respectively.
- 2026-11-04, **Human Motion Prediction via Continual Prior Compensation**, Jianwei Tang et.al., Paper: [@2611.06637](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06637)
> **Abstract:** Human Motion Prediction (HMP) aims to predict future human poses at different moments according to observed past motion sequences. Previous approaches mainly treated the prediction of different temporal moments as a single prediction task and learned the predictions of varied moments simultaneously, which would encounter a main limitation: the learning of short-term predictions (referring to ``near-future'' prediction) could be hindered by the predictions of long-term (referring to ``far-future'' prediction) motions. In this paper, we develop a novel temporal continual learning framework called Continual Prior Compensation (CPC) to progressively train HMP models, in which we divide the prediction task of motions corresponding to varied temporal moments into several subtasks and train the model in a multi-stage manner. To mitigate the prior information forgetting in the progressive training, we further introduce a learnable random variable Prior Compensation Factor (PCF) to explicitly measure the prior knowledge loss. We theoretically show that the PCF can be efficiently learned together with the model parameters by minimizing a reasonable upper bound of the objective function. The proposed CPC is further enhanced to estimate the prior information loss for each subtask and a new framework called Continual Prior Compensation++ (CPC++) with Fine-Grained Prior Compensation Factor (FGPCF) is finally developed. Our CPC and CPC++ frameworks are quite flexible and can be easily integrated with different HMP backbone models and adapted to various datasets and applications. Extensive experiments on three HMP benchmark datasets using multiple SOTA HMP backbones (PGBIG, siMLPe, MotionMixer, and LTD) demonstrate the effectiveness and flexibility of our frameworks.
- 2026-11-03, **Möbius: A Unified Motion-Language Model Bridging Understanding and Generation**, Jiazhi Shu et.al., Paper: [@2611.05279](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05279)
> **Abstract:** We aim to build a truly unified motion model that can understand, generate, and edit human motion within a single framework. Existing motion UMMs remain only partially unified: motion understanding is handled by a language model, while generation is often delegated to a separate diffusion module operating over learned motion latents. We introduce Möbius, which brings the entire diffusion process into the same multimodal representation space used for motion understanding. Clean motion, noisy motion, and text jointly participate in shared attention, while role-specific parameters process understanding and generation streams. Importantly, Möbius operates directly on continuous frame-level raw motion, requiring neither discrete tokenization nor a pretrained motion VAE. This simple formulation naturally supports motion captioning, text-to-motion generation, temporal completion, and instruction-guided editing within a single model. On HumanML3D, Möbius achieves state-of-the-art performance among unified motion models. Scaling to 1.7B parameters and 1,700 hours of motion data further demonstrates that our framework is both effective and scalable.

## Vision-Language & Action Models (VLA/VLM)

- 2026-11-16, **ScaleVLA: Scaling Vision-Language-Action Models to 552 Billion Parameters**, Ruixi Yu et.al., Paper: [@2611.21607](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21607)
> **Abstract:** Frontier Vision-Language Models (VLMs) such as GPT-6 Astra have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in robot control, suggesting that broad pretraining provides knowledge valuable for physical action. Yet most Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies still rely on much smaller VLM backbones, leaving this potential largely untapped. We ask whether frontier-scale representations can provide a stronger foundation for robot learning. We introduce ScaleVLA, which connects a 552B DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash backbone to a trainable action DiT, and systematically compare VLMs from 2B to 552B under a shared training setup. We evaluate three fundamental capabilities of robot foundation models. In sample efficiency, just 10 demonstrations outperform a 2B backbone trained with 50 (87.8% vs. 85.6%). In generalization, LIBERO-PRO success reaches 45.1%, compared with at most 31.3% for smaller backbones. In precise manipulation, real-world charger insertion improves from 4/10 to 9/10 successes, with gains concentrated in contact-sensitive alignment. Despite its scale, ScaleVLA achieves 216 ms median latency per action chunk. Our findings suggest that frontier VLMs can serve not only as reasoning agents, but also as powerful representational foundations for efficient and generalizable robot policies.
- 2026-11-16, **How VLMs Plan What's Next**, Rohit Gandikota et.al., Paper: [@2611.21600](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21600)
> **Abstract:** When a vision-language model describes an image, it names one object at a time and it rarely repeats itself, yet how the model plans this order is far from obvious. We characterize the mechanism a VLM uses to decide where to look before it looks. The mechanism has four main parts. Third, a select step chooses the nearest unwritten region after the cursor, or stops. > **Abstract:** Precipitation nowcasting involves two distinct scales of uncertainty: macroscopic storm evolution and fine-scale local convective variations. Across nine targets, source training raises mean area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) from frozen-model values of 0.821 to 0.912 for Qwen, 0.883 to 0.947 for LLaVA, and 0.939 to 0.962 for InternVL. With five target examples per class and 26 updates per run, 95% confidence intervals for the AUROC benefit over Target-only lie above zero for Qwen, CLIP-temporal, VideoMAE, and Pose-TCN. With full-target training, they lie above zero only for Qwen and LLaVA. Separate VLM analyses reveal threshold sensitivity and post-fall false detections. The benefit varies by model and label budget.

## World Models & Generative Video

- 2026-11-16, **Reference Forcing: Long Video Generation with Region-Guided Drift Correction**, Sheng Zhao et.al., Paper: [@2611.21559](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21559)
> **Abstract:** Long video generation should keep the subject and background consistent while supporting rich, natural motion. However, autoregressive models are often distilled with distribution matching distillation (DMD) over short windows, which does not train them to keep this consistency over long ranges. As a result, the subject and background can drift over time. Methods that manage the key-value (KV) cache retain or retrieve history to reduce drift. Yet this history may itself have drifted. We propose Reference Forcing, which uses the initial frames as an appearance reference. We first build a teacher that uses attention masks to force each region of a new frame to attend to the same region in the initial frames. The teacher finds these regions with off-the-shelf segmentation models. We then distill this behavior into a separate attention path of the student, which needs no external models at inference. We further introduce value correction, which corrects drifted values in the KV cache toward the matching values of drift-free initial frames. Experiments show that Reference Forcing achieves state-of-the-art subject and background consistency among long-video methods on different base models, and its advantage grows with video length. Ablations show both components are needed to gain consistency without losing motion. More visualizations and code are available at https://zhaosheng-thu.github.io/reference-force/
- 2026-11-16, **Lazy-MOSAIC: Accelerating Skill-Centric Manipulation Planning with Learned Skill-Effect World Models**, Itamar Mishani et.al., Paper: [@2611.21517](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21517)
> **Abstract:** Long-horizon manipulation tasks require a robot to perform a sequence of physical interactions with its environment, typically combining several different skills. Planning such behaviors efficiently is challenging: given a library of parameterized skills, solutions lie in a vast hybrid space of skill sequences and their parameters, and evaluating each candidate skill's feasibility and effect with a high-fidelity world model such as a physics simulator is computationally expensive. MOSAIC, a recent skill-centric approach, lets the skills guide a non-sequential search toward the regions of this space where they are likely to succeed; however, it pays the full cost of simulation for every parameterized skill it considers. In this work, we introduce Lazy-MOSAIC, a lazy planning framework that instead searches over hypothesized skill outcomes. A cheap learned skill-effect world model predicts, in a fraction of a millisecond, a skill's feasibility and the world state it produces. Lazy-MOSAIC then uses these predictions to focus expensive simulation on promising candidate skills and, when a hypothesis is invalidated, repairs only the affected part of the search, while ensuring that every skill in a returned plan is validated in simulation. We evaluate Lazy-MOSAIC on cluttered-shelf retrieval tasks of increasing difficulty and demonstrate it on a physical robot, showing that it plans substantially faster than baselines with fewer simulator queries, while matching or exceeding their success rate in every setting. Visit skill-mosaic.github.io.
- 2026-11-16, **WeVisForensics: Self-Evolving Agent for Omni AI-Generated Video Detection**, Yipeng Du et.al., Paper: [@2611.21373](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21373)
> **Abstract:** Recent advances in video generation call for reliable detection grounded in explicit forensic evidence. However, current methods primarily depend on single-pass chain-of-thought reasoning that overlooks brief or localized artifacts, or on predefined multi-agent workflows that offer limited flexibility in selecting relevant inspections. Even with adaptive investigation, optimizing the investigation policy alone does not directly improve the forensic capabilities of fixed experts. We introduce WeVisForensics, an agentic framework in which a main investigator agent coordinates a self-evolving library of forensic inspector sub-agents spanning visual and audio cues. The investigator learns which artifact type to examine, over which window, and when to conclude, and these interactions provide shared feedback for both levels of agents. Our dual-timescale policy optimization strategy, DTPO, closes this loop through fast reinforcement learning of the investigator and slower refinement of the inspectors, guided by annotated artifact intervals, to report subtle artifacts they once dismissed. Furthermore, we curate ForenCue-30K, combining role-specific forensic annotations and interaction trajectories to supervise individual inspections and their coordination, with an accompanying reinforcement-learning pool. Experiments show that WeVisForensics averages 95.2% detection accuracy across in-domain, out-of-domain and agentic benchmarks, 9.0% above the strongest prior detector, while grounding its verdicts in localized evidence.
- 2026-11-16, **MovieRoll: A Systematic Framework for Rolling Out Long-Form Videos**, Yunyang Ge et.al., Paper: [@2611.21339](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21339)
> **Abstract:** Streaming video generation must sustain quality beyond the training horizon while handling self-generated history and growing cache costs. To address these challenges, we present MovieRoll, a base-to-distilled framework that connects streaming fine-tuning, long-rollout distillation, and long-horizon inference. We first establish a robust multi-step base through Window-wise Teacher Forcing (WinTF), which jointly denoises chunks at progressive noise levels with bidirectional attention within each window and causal access to preceding history. To retain historical context efficiently, hierarchical KV-cache compression (HieraKV) exploits layer-wise attention patterns, retaining full history in shallow layers and sink--recent history in deeper layers. This reduces cache memory by up to 78% while improving generation quality. Building on this base, cumulative long tuning with distribution matching distillation (CuLT-DMD) holds parameters fixed throughout each long self-rollout and averages local gradients before updating. Each update therefore combines supervision across temporal positions while retaining only one local computation graph, enabling efficient long-rollout training of a few-step generator. To extend generation beyond the training horizon, KV recomputation bounds context for both models, while guidance annealing (AnnealCFG) reduces guidance toward the clean endpoint to stabilize MovieRoll-Base. Complementing these modeling advances, FBCA attention and PS-VAE decoding support efficient training and streaming output. On HeliosBench, MovieRoll-Base remains competitive at 30s after training on videos of up to 15s, and MovieRoll-Distilled achieves the highest total scores among the compared methods across single- and multi-event evaluations up to 60s, combining sustained naturalness with active motion.
- 2026-11-16, **ScoutCache: Efficient Few-Step Autoregressive Video Diffusion via Conditional Readout and Selective Caching**, Hao Tan et.al., Paper: [@2611.21038](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21038)
> **Abstract:** Few-step autoregressive video diffusion enables efficient long video generation, but inference still incurs substantial computational costs from repeated denoising and KV cache operations. Existing acceleration methods reduce parts of this overhead, while historical KV reading and cache construction remain costly across video chunks. To address these challenges, we propose ScoutCache, a training-free framework that jointly optimizes denoising, historical KV reading, and cache construction. We observe that shallow feature changes provide useful information for estimating clean latent updates, while historical KV dependence varies across Transformer layers and attention heads but remains relatively stable across video chunks. Based on these observations, we develop Conditional Denoising Readout (CDR) to estimate intermediate denoising updates from shallow feature changes and known noise variations. We further introduce Output Risk Guided Historical KV Reading (ORHR) to allocate historical reading budgets based on attention output perturbations, and Content and Coverage Guided Sparse Cache Construction (CCSC) to reduce cache construction costs by selecting informative spatial tokens while maintaining spatial coverage. - 2026-11-16, **Sparse Attention for Visual Generation: Toward Algorithm-System Co-Design**, Majun Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.21029](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21029)
> **Abstract:** Rapid advances in image and video generation have enabled high-fidelity synthesis at higher resolutions and longer durations, making the quadratic cost of dense self-attention a major bottleneck as visual token sequences grow. Sparse attention seeks to preserve generation quality at lower computational cost by retaining informative query-key interactions and omitting less consequential ones, with end-to-end gains depending on the cost of constructing sparse support and the efficiency with which the system maps the resulting workload to hardware. We survey sparse attention for visual generation through a 4+1+3 analytical framework that links algorithmic design to system implementation. The framework examines how static and dynamic methods use source information and the evidence derived from it to decide which interactions to retain and at what granularity, while accounting for the roles of reuse in reducing repeated computation and correction in compensating for omitted contributions. We relate these algorithmic choices to their implementation on hardware through the framework's three system dimensions of data organization, execution, and dataflow. We summarize common evaluation metrics and discuss open challenges and research directions, providing researchers and practitioners with a reference for the joint design of sparse attention algorithms and systems.
- 2026-11-16, **SciWorldBench: Do Video World Models Really Understand Scientific Experiments?**, Xue Yang et.al., Paper: [@2611.20899](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20899)
> **Abstract:** While the ability of video generation models to accurately depict scientific phenomena has received growing attention, determining whether these models genuinely understand the underlying principles remains a critical challenge. Existing benchmarks typically evaluate video samples in isolation, making it difficult to expose models that simply fit superficial visual priors. In scientific experiments, a small change in one condition produces a markedly different outcome even when the rest of the setup remains fixed. Capitalizing on this sensitivity, we introduce SciWorldBench, a benchmark comprising paired scientific experiments. Each pair varies a single condition in an otherwise fixed setup, yielding two mutually exclusive expected outcomes. Each generation prompt specifies the condition but omits the expected outcome. We evaluate ten text-and-image-to-video (TI2V) models using an independent vision-language model (VLM) that assesses each video without access to the condition text or the label identifying the correct outcome. A pair is scored as correct only when both videos depict their respective scientifically correct outcomes. Across all ten models, we find that the two videos in an experimental pair frequently depict the same outcome even though the conditions require different results. This recurring failure suggests that current video world models struggle to apply the underlying scientific principles reliably during generation. We will publicly release the benchmark dataset and open-source the evaluation code.
- 2026-11-16, **Auditing World-Model Explanations in Retail Forecasting: Faithfulness, Accuracy and Price Response**, Kathik Iyer et.al., Paper: [@2611.20811](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20811)
> **Abstract:** Forecasting models that read the price and promotion plan explain each forecast as a baseline, what would sell with nothing planned, plus the lift the plan adds. We test such explanations with eight checks, six with a pass rule fixed before any result, and find that passing them does not make an explanation right. In generated store data with known effects, a world model passes the checks of construction, stability, price direction and agreement with sales and with LightGBM, yet its lifts are 1.33 to 1.40 times the truth when merchants plan into high-demand weeks, and 1.23 to 1.42 times in eight variants of that world. It still picks the right weeks to promote, but stock ordered to its lift is off by 0.44 to 0.48 of the true lift. Its stated response to a lasting regular-price change is 4.7 to 6.5 times the true -0.5, and only a measured cause finds this. On Dominick's, 38 episodes in which a store's regular prices sat apart from its zone peers give a noisy benchmark: a pooled 8-week response of -0.33 (95% interval -0.68 to 0.01). For the final tuned world models of our companion paper on nine data sets, baseline and lift add up under each model's own plan, but a deeper price cut lowers the forecast in 29% to 83% of item-week steps on Dominick's (23% to 80% within prices seen in training), and the world model and LightGBM both state about twice the measured response. Across 703 configurations from the settings searches, checks passed were not related to forecast accuracy (Spearman 0.02, 95% interval -0.02 to 0.05); with no known answer for those versions, this concerns accuracy, while whether an explanation is right is measured only in the generated data.
- 2026-11-16, **QASK-Edit: Adaptive Sparse Attention Across Queries and Key Sources for Efficient Video Editing**, Hengrui Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.20704](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20704)
> **Abstract:** Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) have achieved strong performance in generation based video editing, but suffer from high inference latency due to the long token sequences formed by conditioning inputs and generated videos. Although training-free sparse attention reduces this cost, existing methods typically apply a fixed retention ratio or a single threshold shared across all tokens. In this work, we view video editing as conditional generation and identify a key insight: attention demand varies significantly both across different key sources (e.g., source videos, reference images, audio, text, and generated videos) and across queries within each source. Motivated by this, we propose QASK-Edit (Query-Adaptive Sparsity over Key Sources for Video Editing), a training-free sparse attention framework that allocates computation independently for each query and key source. Specifically, for each query, QASK-Edit considers both the attention mass and approximation risk of each block. > **Abstract:** Directly aligning trajectory-distilled video generators slashes rollout costs and optimizes the deployed few-step model without teacher fine-tuning or re-distillation. However, conventional flow-matching preference objectives assume single-head local velocity supervision, mismatching the finite-interval, block-average transition interface of trajectory-distilled generators such as Parallel Decoding Distillation (PDD) and Parametric Trajectory Distillation (PTD). - 2026-11-15, **PoCoAttn: Position-Content Collaborative Sparse Attention for Fast and High-Fidelity Video Generation**, Jianwen Wu et.al., Paper: [@2611.20307](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20307)
> **Abstract:** Video diffusion transformers rely on 3D full attention over tens of thousands of spatiotemporal tokens, and its quadratic cost dominates inference latency. - 2026-11-15, **SAFER: Navigating the Exploration-Exploitation Dilemma in World-Model-Based Planning for Autonomous Driving**, Yansong Qu et.al., Paper: [@2611.20107](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20107)
> **Abstract:** An autonomous vehicle must foresee the consequences of its actions, especially in the rare, time-critical situations where crashes happen. A world model offers this foresight, but only within the coverage of its training data, which poses an exploration-exploitation dilemma. Within this coverage a planner can hardly improve on the data, and beyond it the model's errors mislead it. In planning, a chunked latent world model Foresees the consequences fast enough for real-time use, and a risk-aware sampling-based planner Evaluates candidate maneuvers by their return minus a bounded collision penalty. - 2026-11-15, **Latent World Modeling for Point-Cloud Video Pretraining**, Zhuoyue Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.20090](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20090)
> **Abstract:** Masked modeling is the dominant self-supervised paradigm for point-cloud videos. > **Abstract:** Driving world models usually generate videos from six pinhole cameras with separate optical centres and small overlaps, and must learn to keep these views consistent. > **Abstract:** Video world models promise to predict the future of a scene from its present. Do current models keep this promise? We probe this question counterfactually. > **Abstract:** Latent world models predict the future in an embedding space rather than in pixels. > **Abstract:** For more than half a century, video games have pursued better graphics, more natural interaction, and more engaging gameplay. Generative video models (VMs) now bear on all three. These systems, however, treat the VM as a renderer that only restyles motion the engine has already authored, which leaves most of its generative capacity unused and limits the diversity a game can offer. We present GameWM, a design for next-generation games built on a clear division of labor. The GameWM-engine keeps what must be exact: rules, state, camera, and the layout of the world, which also spares the video model from having to remember it. The GameWM-VM generates what is expensive to author by hand: the look of the world and the detail of the player's interactions. One game mechanic can then be realized in many visually and physically distinct ways, while its outcome stays exactly as the engine computed it. To realize this division of labor, we build GameWM as a complete system: a data engine centered on interactions rather than exploration, an interface through which authored character sheets, scene art, and skill descriptions enter the model, and a model family from 5B to 33B parameters with a real-time causal variant for play. Together they give every player a fresh version of the same game: each player's own character, world, and skills turn the same engine mechanics into a new experience, while every event keeps the timing and outcome the GameWM-engine computed.

- 2026-11-08, **Semantically Aligned KV Cache Compression for Efficient Agentic Autoregressive Video Generation**, Weizhong Huang et.al., Paper: [@2611.11452](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11452)
> **Abstract:** Agentic autoregressive video generation combines semantic planning, sequential generation, and visual verification for controllable synthesis. Growing visual histories enlarge the key-value (KV) cache, increasing memory use and attention costs across candidate rollouts. Existing compression methods primarily optimize individual generators without the planner's semantic constraints, risking low priority for states supporting requested attributes or actions. We introduce SemanticKV, a training-free method for semantically aligned KV cache compression. To our knowledge, SemanticKV is the first KV cache compression method for agentic autoregressive video generation that explicitly uses planner-extracted semantic constraints to guide retention. Text cross-attention identifies constraint-related video positions, whose self-attention queries are collected into separate groups. Attention weights are averaged within each group and maximized across groups, giving each cached entry a high score when strongly used by any represented constraint. A single top-k selection retains high-scoring entries under the memory budget, leaving their keys, values, and positions unchanged. For fixed query groups and equal entry costs, this selection minimizes an additive upper bound on worst-group discarded attention. The method operates independently within each candidate without retraining or additional video generations. We evaluate four AR video backbones on three benchmarks for 5-, 30-, and 60-second generation. > **Abstract:** Aligning video generation models with human preference depends critically on the quality of the reward model that scores generated videos. Current video reward models, however, source their reward signal from a model's representational rather than generative capacity: they either repurpose vision-language models as pixel-space scorers, or train auxiliary scoring heads on top of frozen generator features. The generator's actual ability to predict velocities, denoise latents, and produce videos plays no role in scoring its own generations. We argue that this overlooks a structural property of offline preference learning. Any preference optimization objective that approximates an offline preference dataset mathematically targets a strictly defined KL-constrained optimal policy. By isolating the reward term from this analytical solution and mapping the exact generation log-likelihood to its flow-matching surrogate, the empirical reward emerges deterministically as the relative difference in continuous-time velocity-prediction errors. We formalize this equivalence into GenRM-Flow, a framework where a preference-tuned video generator inherently operates as a process-aware reward model directly in the noisy latent space. We further show theoretically that distinct fine-tuning objectives, whether contrastive like DPO and IPO or non-contrastive like RWR, are alternative optimization pathways toward the same optimal policy, each inducing a reward readout from the generator's velocity-prediction errors. - 2026-11-08, **TransArt3D: From Two States to Articulated 3D Reconstruction with Video Transition Priors**, Chenhao Guan et.al., Paper: [@2611.11304](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11304)
> **Abstract:** Reconstructing interactable articulated assets from a few images is key to robotics and simulation, yet highly underconstrained. - 2026-11-07, **The Delta Gap: Cellular World Models for Perturbation Response Prediction Are Far Harder Than Global Correlation Implies**, Junhui Gao et.al., Paper: [@2611.10658](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10658)

- 2026-11-07, **Action Transfer Across Character Body Plans in Latent-Action World Models: An Empirical Study of Animated Characters**, Amarnath R et.al., Paper: [@2611.10523](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10523)
> **Abstract:** Latent-action world models learn actions from video without labels, and recent methods aim to share these actions across bodies. - 2026-11-07, **ESTRA: Enabling Extreme Attention Sparsity for Training-Free Video Diffusion Acceleration**, Enhuai Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.10448](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10448)
> **Abstract:** Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but attention over long spatiotemporal sequences makes inference computationally expensive. ## Computational Design, Mechanism & Fabrication

- 2026-11-10, **Optimization-Based Path Planning for Industrial Manipulators: Experimental Validation in Additive Manufacturing**, Shiva Ganesh Murali et.al., Paper: [@2611.13983](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13983)
> **Abstract:** Robotic additive manufacturing requires joint paths that reconcile robot feasibility with process-specific geometry. We develop an offline optimization-based path planner that combines joint-path regularity and self-collision penalties with process costs and constraints, so that a new process enters through its own reference, residuals, and tolerances. Full-path and boundary-coupled chunked optimization handle references of different lengths. Iterative Weight Tuning (IWT) determines the cost weights from the gaps between Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) and their targets. In carbon-fiber winding and robotic 3D printing, the planned paths meet the geometric tolerances and all KPI targets, and both paths are executed on a UR10e, without extrusion for printing, with joint-level tracking recorded during execution.
- 2026-11-09, **A Unified Interactive Framework for Real-Time Three-Dimensional Cellular Automata Simulation in XR**, Ashkan Rezaei et.al., Paper: [@2611.13296](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13296)
> **Abstract:** Integrating three-dimensional cellular automata (3D CA) into extended reality (XR) requires coordinated simulation, volumetric data management, rendering, and interaction under variable hardware constraints. This paper presents a hybrid study combining a critical review of existing approaches with a unified architecture for real-time 3D CA in XR. The proposed five-layer architecture integrates voxel and rule management, parallel computation, interoperability middleware, visualization, and XR interaction. Its key principles include hardware-aware execution, asynchronous simulation-rendering, adaptive fidelity, and cross-engine interoperability. An analytical evaluation framework is defined to assess performance, latency, scalability, memory efficiency, simulation correctness, and interaction quality. The proposed framework provides a foundation for developing portable and interactive 3D CA systems across desktop and mobile XR platforms.
- 2026-11-09, **Same Physics, Different Gradients: A State-of-the-Art Report on Differentiable Physics Simulation**, Lei Shu et.al., Paper: [@2611.12162](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12162)
> **Abstract:** Differentiable simulation propagates derivatives through a physics simulator, turning animation, system identification, control, and computational design into gradient-based optimization. Engines differ in the physics they model and, less visibly, in which mathematical object they differentiate. We separate two questions the literature conflates. The referent is which object the returned derivative belongs to: the finite computation that executed, the converged equation it approximates, or the continuous dynamics. The realization describes the organization of the backward pass: automatically generated differentiation, an equation-level adjoint solve, or a custom adjoint. The referent defines the target against which exactness is assessed; the implementation determines how accurately that target is approximated. Cost depends on the realization together with how the states needed by the backward pass are retained or recovered. Crossing the axes places every method and engine we classify in a cell, separates claims the literature merges, and shows that the apparent trade-off between exactness and memory follows from how methods have been built rather than from a constraint. A second, orthogonal taxonomy covers contact, where the map is non-smooth. We then ask whether a correct gradient helps. Four mechanisms by which useful gradients are lost are cataloged and measured on one testbed, where the advantage of the exact derivative over the equation-level adjoint depends on where the fitting target comes from as much as on the solver budget, and we review the evidence that zeroth-order methods can outperform simulator gradients in the regimes where those mechanisms act. The model classes, systems techniques and applications are surveyed through this lens, and we close with a reporting checklist, a released diagnostic that computes it, and a research agenda.
- 2026-11-09, **Design of a hybrid tendon-linkage finger with coupled and adaptive motion**, Hyojae Kang et.al., Paper: [@2611.11885](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11885)
> **Abstract:** Dexterous robotic fingers require coordinated joint motion and adaptability to object contact, while independently actuating multiple joints increases the complexity of the transmission mechanism. This paper presents a hybrid tendon--linkage robotic finger with four mechanical degrees of freedom driven by three actuators. The MCP abduction/adduction joint is driven through a bevel gear, while MCP and PIP flexion/extension are driven by separate bidirectional tendon transmissions. The DIP joint is not independently actuated but is coupled to the PIP joint through a four-bar linkage with a torsional spring and mechanical stopper. The tendon routing also introduces mechanical coupling between the MCP and PIP joints. ArUco-based measurements showed that the coupled motion closely matched the theoretical prediction, whereas the adaptive motion was partially limited by interference between linkage components. The fabricated finger demonstrated different contact postures with cylindrical, cuboid, and irregularly shaped objects, and the mean peak fingertip force was 4.56 N. - 2026-11-06, **Segmenting Geometric Textures of 3D Meshes**, Weidan Xiong et.al., Paper: [@2611.08897](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08897)
> **Abstract:** Recognizing geometric texture patterns on 3D models is critical for applications such as heritage protection, model editing, and 3D printing. Existing 3D model segmentation methods predominantly focus on identifying high-level semantic parts, thereby failing to capture middle-level textures. While recent Vision-Language Models can perform image segmentation with appropriate text prompts, they still struggle to recognize 3D geometric textures. In this work, we present a method that segments 3D mesh models based on middle-level geometric textures. ## Sketch-based & Creative HCI Tools

- 2026-11-16, **Shaping AI's Beliefs Before It Generates: Human Sense of Agency in Creative AI**, Zi Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.21405](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21405)
> **Abstract:** AI systems for creative media generation expand human capabilities yet risk diminishing human agency. A core problem is anchoring, where the user's thinking becomes anchored to a polished, generated artifact, shifting the user's role from active authoring to reactive editing. We propose pre-generation belief controls that can empower creators to inspect, revise, and steer the AI's interpretation before any media is generated, so that the initial anchor is set by the human. We formalize four design principles and instantiate them in the Proactive Co-Creator (PaCC), a system for early-stage conceptual ideation and prototyping that proactively surfaces its assumptions and uncertainty through clarifications, belief graphs, and media attributes, inviting users to shape the AI's beliefs before generating the final outcome. Using PaCC as a design probe in a small-scale, single-arm exploratory user study, we observed that participants reported directionally positive sense of agency and process control relative to their familiar prompt-based AI tools, noting that surfaced assumptions helped them think through details they had overlooked, whereas their feelings about ownership and authorship diverged
- 2026-11-16, **PromptPoseFX: Authoring Pose-Driven Visual Effects through Augmented Prompting and Interactive Refinement**, Kexin Zheng et.al., Paper: [@2611.20485](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20485)
> **Abstract:** Human pose-driven visual effects offer a compelling way for enhancing video expressiveness and storytelling. Authoring such effects with traditional video editing tools is a complex and time-consuming task that requires considerable technical expertise and manual effort. While generative AI models can simplify the workflow by reducing manual scripting through language-driven generation, existing approaches suffer from imprecise alignment between visual effects and pose data, failure to preserve original video content, and limited support for fine-grained, iterative refinement. To address these challenges, we present PromptPoseFX, an interactive system that allows creators to author pose-driven visual effects through augmented language prompts and interactive refinement. Our system leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to translate high-level user intent into parameterized procedural graphics scripts, automatically mapping pose-tracking data to specific effect parameters, allowing precise alignment with human motion. This script-based approach manages effects as independent layers, thereby preserving the original content of the underlying video. To reduce language ambiguity, our interface offers controls across spatial, temporal, and parameter domains, enabling the precise, intuitive, and iterative authoring of pose-driven visual effects. We evaluated PromptPoseFX through a comparative study against a text-only LLM interface, followed by an exploratory study. In the comparative study, participants rated PromptPoseFX significantly higher than the text-only baseline on measures of perceived control and precision. The exploratory study examined usability and creativity support in open-ended authoring tasks.
- 2026-11-13, **DesignSpecs: A DESIGN.md Dataset of Visual Design Specifications for AI Agents**, Jialiang Wei et.al., Paper: [@2611.17713](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17713)
> **Abstract:** While AI agents increasingly generate and modify user interfaces, they rarely rely on a visual design specification for a certain project. The DESIGN$.$md format, which originated from Google's AI design tool Stitch and which was open-sourced in April 2026, addresses this gap by describing a visual identity through design tokens (such as named colors, text styles) and Markdown prose. To our knowledge, no large-scale research dataset of such design specification files exists. We present DesignSpecs, a dataset of 36,906 DESIGN$.$md files from 15,794 public, non-fork GitHub repositories, representing 17,240 unique file contents. We collected 613,207 files named DESIGN$.$md in October 2026. Because the same file name also denotes architectural plans and other design documents, we filtered those with YAML front matter, a design-system name, at least one recognized design token, and zero errors under the official linter. Our JSON Lines dataset provides file-level and deduplicated views of the original Markdown, links files to repositories, paths, and commits, and is released with related scripts. We outline research opportunities on visual design knowledge and design-aware AI agents. Dataset is available at: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Jl-wei/DesignSpecs
- 2026-11-09, **Creative Intent as an Evolving Shared State: Supporting Cross-Stage Human-AI Video Co-Creation**, Yuhang Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.12605](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12605)
> **Abstract:** As generative AI becomes increasingly involved in video creation, creators face challenges in expanding, translating, and negotiating creative intent across stages. We therefore view creative intent as an evolving shared state throughout human-AI co-creation. A multi-stage design was employed: semi-structured interviews with 12 creators informed 3 design goals, followed by expert co-design and the development of VideoMaker. VideoMaker organizes creation into a six-stage workflow that progressively translates creative intent. At key stages, creators negotiate with AI through the Seed Panel, explicitly confirming, revising, and updating intent. A shared AI Agent, supported by the Multi-style Script Library and Cinematic Language Knowledge Base, enables differentiated expansion of initial intent and faithful translation. A within-subjects study with 30 participants showed that VideoMaker effectively supported the expansion and cross-stage translation of creative intent, with stronger intent preservation, while negotiated boundaries between creator-confirmed intent and AI initiative remained difficult to sustain.
- 2026-11-09, **Co-Creating Neurorehabilitation Technology Alongside Lived Experience Experts: A Participatory Design Workshop**, Irene L. Y. Beck et.al., Paper: [@2611.12202](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12202)
> **Abstract:** Involving patients' lived experience (LE) early in neurorehabilitation technology design can improve the relevance and effectiveness of solutions, yet LE experts are often involved only when finished prototypes are tested. We present and evaluate a participatory design (PD) workshop for early LE involvement, examine how LE contributions influence design processes and outcomes, and provide recommendations for similar approaches. We conducted a PD workshop at the 6th International Conference on NeuroRehabilitation. Following the Discover, Define, and Develop phases of the Double Diamond model, each group used Journey Maps and Data Collection Notebooks to co-create a solution for an objective specified by an LE expert. Journey Maps were thematically analyzed, and group audio transcripts underwent structured extraction using a large language model. LE input shifted discussions from discipline-based assumptions toward priorities grounded in lived experience, including fear, confidence, sensory discomfort, fatigue, aesthetics, and independence. Narrow objectives, such as opening a blister pack, produced task-specific solutions, whereas broader objectives generated device concepts, including sensory feedback for cycling and a stimulation-equipped cooking splint. Satisfaction was high among attendees and LE experts. All workshop tools and protocols are shared to support adoption. The workshop offers a practical foundation for involving LE experts early in neurorehabilitation technology development. Future iterations should better capture psychological and social dimensions, allow more reflection, and include the full range of relevant stakeholders through sustained transdisciplinary engagement between researchers and the LE community.
- 2026-11-08, **From Sketch to Design: A Cross-scale Workflow for Procedural Generative Urban Design**, Jin Gao et.al., Paper: [@2611.11561](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11561)
> **Abstract:** Urban designers use sketches and symbols to express spatial intentions, while many generative urban design tools rely on numeric parameters that are difficult to relate to this visual process. We propose a cross-scale workflow and a web-based prototype that translates symbolic sketches into controllable procedural urban generation. A vocabulary of shapes, informed by examples of urban design diagrams and sketching exercises with graduate urban design students, encodes attributes such as network structure, density, and program. Implemented in JavaScript and TypeScript with React and Three.js, the prototype supports interactive sketching, visualization, and urban massing generation. We describe the methodology and illustrate prototype interactions, then discuss downstream integration with architectural-scale generative tools for further refinement and analysis. - 2026-11-03, **OmniScribble: Mixed-Initiative Authoring of Animated Sketches through Inspectable and Repairable AI Decisions**, Hien Trinh et.al., Paper: [@2611.04284](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04284)
> **Abstract:** AI-assisted creative tools reduce the effort of authoring content, but delegating creative decisions to AI can make it difficult for authors to understand, correct, or redirect work. ## 3D Understanding: Reconstruction & Generation

- 2026-11-16, **The Mask Is Not the Object: Volumetric Supervision for 3D Gaussian Segmentation**, Jeonghwan Cho et.al., Paper: [@2611.21383](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21383)
> **Abstract:** 3D Gaussian Splatting enables object-level operations on Gaussian primitives, yet existing segmentation methods are largely trained and evaluated through rendered views. This mismatch can produce accurate rendered masks from incomplete 3D selections since alpha-blended feature supervision concentrates learning on dominant contributors while leaving occluded or low-contribution Gaussians weakly supervised. We propose VODA, a plug-and-play supervision objective that exploits local depth structure to group per-pixel contributors and directly supervises all Gaussians in selected depth clusters, bypassing contribution-weighted gradient delivery. To evaluate volumetric completeness, we introduce Removal-3D, a synthetic object-removal benchmark, and the Removal Depth Error (RDE), a metric comparing predicted and ground-truth depth changes. Across four rendering-based baselines, VODA consistently improves volumetric segmentation on Removal-3D while maintaining or improving performance on LERF and ScanNet. Further analysis shows that VODA recovers lower-contribution Gaussians, while simple gradient reweighting and spatial propagation fail to reproduce comparable gains.
- 2026-11-16, **Déjà-Events: Multi-view 3D Geometry with Event Cameras via Sparsity-Aware Adaptive Gating**, Arda Cinar Demirtas et.al., Paper: [@2611.21302](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21302)
> **Abstract:** Event cameras keep capturing structure where conventional cameras blur or saturate, which makes them attractive for 3D perception in fast or poorly lit scenes. We present Déjà-Events, an event-only model that predicts depth, camera pose and pointmaps from multiple views in a single forward pass. Rather than relying on scarce 3D labels, we distil it from Déjà View, a feed-forward RGB model that refines its tokens by repeatedly applying one shared transformer block. On paired RGB and event recordings, the event student learns to reproduce the frozen teacher's final tokens, which requires no depth or pose ground truth and trains only about 1% of its parameters. Because events are dense at edges and moving texture but nearly absent in textureless regions, we further introduce sparsity-aware adaptive gating, which adapts the shared block's gates to events and adjusts them per patch by event density, so that accuracy holds as refinement steps are added. Trained only on simulated data, Déjà-Events achieves the lowest pointmap error among the event-based methods we evaluate, on EventScape and zero-shot on the real MVSEC and DSEC datasets. On DSEC it halves the error of the strongest baseline (0.15 vs. 0.30 AbsRel) with about a tenth of its parameters. It also has the lowest depth error on EventScape and DSEC and the lowest trajectory error on EventScape.
- 2026-11-16, **QuantileGS: Quantile-Based Conformal Bounds on RGB Rendering Errors for Novel View Synthesis with 3D Gaussian Splatting**, Haiyang Xu et.al., Paper: [@2611.21253](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21253)
> **Abstract:** Uncertainty quantification for novel view synthesis with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) assesses rendering reliability at unseen viewpoints. This reliability depends on the discrepancy between rendered and true colors, which cannot be measured without ground-truth images. We therefore quantify uncertainty in this unknown discrepancy through upper bounds on per-pixel RGB mean absolute error. In this paper, we propose QuantileGS, a post-hoc framework that combines learned rendering-error quantiles with scale-normalized conformal calibration to produce spatially adaptive error bounds. The framework comprises three phases. Phase 1, Rendering-Error Computation, measures RGB mean absolute error between observed images and predictions from a frozen 3DGS model. Phase 2, Ordered Gaussian Quantile Fields, learns two scalar attributes per Gaussian to predict lower and upper rendering-error quantiles. Phase 3, Scale-Normalized Conformal Calibration, calibrates the upper prediction on held-out images, using the quantile gap to scale the correction. Under the stated assumptions, the resulting bounds provide finite-sample marginal coverage. At a nominal coverage level of 90%, calibration raises mean empirical test coverage from 69.6% to 89.6% on LLFF and from 80.1% to 92.1% on Mip-NeRF 360.
